The public surface is\nexactly four names:\n\n* ``validate(instance, schema, path=\"$\")`` -> a list of human-readable\n  violation strings, empty when the instance satisfies the schema;\n* ``UnsupportedKeyword``, an Exception;\n* ``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS`` and ``ANNOTATION_KEYWORDS``, two disjoint non-empty\n  frozensets.\n\nA schema keyword that is neither enforced (``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS``) nor\nknowingly constraint-free (``ANNOTATION_KEYWORDS``) raises\n``UnsupportedKeyword``, naming the keyword. A supported keyword in a form this\nchecker does not enforce raises the same way. The audit is eager and total: it\nwalks the whole schema, descending into ``allOf`` and ``oneOf`` branches,\n``not``, ``if`` and ``then``, ``propertyNames``, an ``additionalProperties``\nsubschema, and every ``$defs`` definition whether referenced or not, so a\nsubschema no instance reaches still raises.\n\nComposition is enforced:\n\n* ``$defs`` / ``$ref`` -- internal pointers of the form ``#/...`` only. An\n  external ``$ref`` cannot be fetched and a dangling one is a broken pointer,\n  not \"no constraint\", so either raises.\n* ``allOf`` -- every branch holds, and every branch's violations are reported.\n* ``if`` / ``then`` -- ``then`` binds only when ``if`` is satisfied; a failing\n  ``if`` is a condition, not a violation.\n* ``oneOf`` -- exactly one branch holds; matching none is distinguishable from\n  matching several.\n* ``not`` -- the subschema must not hold.\n* ``propertyNames`` -- every key of an object satisfies a subschema.\n* ``minItems``, ``minProperties``, ``maximum`` -- the obvious bounds, with\n  ``maximum`` mirroring ``minimum`` and neither applied to a boolean.\n* ``additionalProperties`` in its subschema form -- every property ``properties``\n  does not name must satisfy that subschema; ``False`` still forbids extras and\n  names each one, ``True`` and absent still constrain nothing.\n\nThe governing rule does not move: a construct the checker cannot enforce raises\nrather than passing.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport re\n\nSUPPORTED_KEYWORDS = frozenset({\n    \"type\", \"required\",   \"object\": dict,\n    \"array\": list,\n    \"string\": str,\n    \"integer\": int,\n    \"number\": (int, float),\n    \"boolean\": bool,\n}\n\n\ndef _resolve_pointer(document, ref):\n \"external\" and \"dangling\" identically: both\n    are constructs this checker cannot honestly apply.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref.startswith(\"#/\"):\n        return None\n    node = document\n    for part in ref[2:].split(\"/\"):\n        part = part.replace(\"~1\", \"/\").replace(\"~0\", \"~\")\n        if isinstance(node, dict) and part in node:\n            node = node[part]\n        elif (isinstance(node, list) and part.isdigit()\n              and int(part) < len(node)):\n            node = node[int(part)]\n        else:\n            return None\n    return node\n\n\ndef _audit_schema(schema, path, root, seen=None):\n    \"\"\"Walk the whole schema eagerly, raising on anything unenforceable.\n\n    ``root`` is the top-level schema document that internal ``$ref`` pointers\n    resolve against. ``seen`` guards the recursion against a ``$defs``\n    definition that points back at itself; a node already audited is a node\n    already known to be honest.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(schema, bool):\n        raise UnsupportedKeyword(f\"{path}: boolean schema is not supported\")\n    if not isinstance(schema, dict):\n        raise   return\n    seen.add(marker)\n    for keyword, value in schema.items():\n        if keyword in ANNOTATION_KEYWORDS:\n            continue\n        if keyword not in SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS:\n            raise UnsupportedKeyword(f\"{path}: unsupported keyword {keyword!r}\")\n        if keyword == \"type\":\n            if not isinstance(value, str):\n                raise UnsupportedKeyword(\n                    f\"{path}: type as a union list is not supported\")\n        elif keyword == \"items\":\n            if isinstance(value, list):\n                raise UnsupportedKeyword(\n                    f\"{path}: items as a positional list is not supported\")\n            _audit_schema(value, f\"{path}.items\", root, seen)\n        elif keyword == \"properties\":\n            for prop_name, \"additionalProperties\":\n            if isinstance(value, dict):\n                _audit_schema(value, f\"{path}.additionalProperties\", root, seen)\n            elif not isinstance(value, bool):\n                raise UnsupportedKeyword(\n                    f\"{path}: additionalProperties must be a boolean or a \"\n                    f\"subschema\")\n        elif keyword == \"$defs\":\n            for name, definition in value.items():\n                _audit_schema(definition, f\"{path}.$defs.{name}\", root, seen)\n        elif keyword == \"$ref\":\n            target = raise UnsupportedKeyword(\n                    f\"{path}: {keyword} must be a list of schemas\")\n            for index, subschema in enumerate(value):\n                _audit_schema(subschema, For a gate, raising is right.\n18: For a human fixing a schema, one anonymous exception per run is close to\n19: useless.\n20: \n21: So the module grows one name::\n22: \n23:     def audit_schema(schema, path=\"$\") -> list[str]\n24: \n25: It reports *every* construct the checker cannot enforce, one entry each, and\n26: raises nothing. Each entry carries the breadcrumb of the offending node\n27: (``$.properties.routing.properties.action_classes``) and names the offending\n28: keyword. An empty list means the whole schema is enforceable.\n29: \n30: The sharpest claim in this file is the agreement between the two::\n31: \n32:     audit_schema(s) == []   if and only if   validate(instance, s) does not raise\n33: \n34: pinned in *both* directions over a table of schemas -- clean ones, ones with a\n35: single defect, ones with several, and the two real schema files in the repo.\n36: An implementation where one drifts from the other reintroduces exactly the\n37: silence this module exists to prevent, in a new place. How the two share code\n38: is deliberately not pinned: an implementer may build one on the other or not.\n39: \n40: Conventions follow the two sibling oracles (``test_schema_check.py`` and\n41: ``test_schema_check_composition.py``, both of which must keep passing): the\n42: subject is loaded with importlib, and nothing is asserted about message\n43: *wording* -- only breadcrumbs and offending keyword names, via ``assertIn``.\n44: \n45: Three deliberate choices, stated so the next reader does not have to guess:\n46: \n47: 1. **No ordering is pinned.** Entry order is not asserted anywhere; every\n48:    assertion is membership or count. There is no defensible order for a walk\n49:    over an unordered document, and pinning one would forbid an implementation\n50:    that walks breadth-first or collects defects out of a queue.\n51: \n52: 2. **Breadcrumbs use the grammar the module's own raise messages already\n53:    use** -- ``$.properties.a``, ``$.items``, ``$.$defs.orphan``,\n54:    ``$.allOf[0]``, ``$.not``, ``$.propertyNames``,\n55:    ``$.additionalProperties``. That grammar is already load-bearing in the\n56:    exception text this row leaves alone, so the audit reusing it is the only\n57:    reading that keeps the two surfaces legible together. The one place it is\n58:    *not* pinned is a node reached through ``$ref``, where the module has two\n59:    equally honest breadcrumbs for one node; there this file asserts only that\n60:    the definition's name appears.\n61: \n62: 3. **A bare boolean in schema position has no keyword to name**, so those\n63:    entries are pinned on the breadcrumb and the count alone, never on a word.\n64: \n65: Keyword *names* that some later row might legitimately implement (``anyOf``,\n66: ``patternProperties``, ``maxItems``, ``contains``, ``maxLength``) are\n67: conditioned on ``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS`` membership, as the sibling oracles do, so\n68: that row can land without editing this file. For a gate, raising is right.\nFor a human fixing a schema, one anonymous exception per run is close to\nuseless.\n\nSo the module grows one name::\n\n    def audit_schema(schema, path=\"$\") -> list[str]\n\nIt reports *every* construct the checker cannot enforce, one entry each, and\nraises nothing. Each entry carries the breadcrumb of the offending node\n(``$.properties.routing.properties.action_classes``) and names the offending\nkeyword. An empty list means the whole schema is enforceable.\n\nThe sharpest claim in this file is the agreement between the two::\n\n    audit_schema(s) == []   if and only if   validate(instance, s) does not raise\n\npinned in *both* directions over a table of schemas -- clean ones, ones with a\nsingle defect, ones with several, and the two real schema files in the repo.\nAn implementation where one drifts from the other reintroduces exactly the\nsilence this module exists to prevent, in a new place. How the two share code\nis deliberately not pinned: an implementer may build one on the other or not.\n\nConventions follow the two sibling oracles (``test_schema_check.py`` and\n``test_schema_check_composition.py``, both of which must keep passing): the\nsubject is loaded with importlib, and nothing is asserted about message\n*wording* -- only breadcrumbs and offending keyword names, via ``assertIn``.\n\nThree deliberate choices, stated so the next reader does not have to guess:\n\n1. **No ordering is pinned.** Entry order is not asserted anywhere; every\n   assertion is membership or count. There is no defensible order for a walk\n   over an unordered document, and pinning one would forbid an implementation\n   that walks breadth-first or collects defects out of a queue.\n\n2. **Breadcrumbs use the grammar the module's own raise messages already\n   use** -- ``$.properties.a``, ``$.items``, ``$.$defs.orphan``,\n   ``$.allOf[0]``, ``$.not``, ``$.propertyNames``,\n   ``$.additionalProperties``. That grammar is already load-bearing in the\n   exception text this row leaves alone, so the audit reusing it is the only\n   reading that keeps the two surfaces legible together. The one place it is\n   *not* pinned is a node reached through ``$ref``, where the module has two\n   equally honest breadcrumbs for one node; there this file asserts only that\n   the definition's name appears.\n\n3. **A bare boolean in schema position has no keyword to name**, so those\n   entries are pinned on the breadcrumb and the count alone, never on a word.\n\nKeyword *names* that some later row might legitimately implement (``anyOf``,\n``patternProperties``, ``maxItems``, ``contains``, ``maxLength``) are\nconditioned on ``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS`` membership, as the sibling oracles do, so\nthat row can land without editing this file. Unenforceable *forms* of already\nsupported keywords are pinned unconditionally, as the composition oracle pins\nthem.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport copy\nimport False\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# The real schema nodes this row was written for.\n# An implementation where\n    one drifts from the other reintroduces exactly the silence this module\n    exists to prevent, in a new place: a schema the gate accepts but the audit\n    condemns is a false alarm, and a schema the audit calls clean but the gate\n    refuses is a fix the human cannot find.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def _all_schemas(self):\n        for name, schema in CLEAN_SCHEMAS.items():\n            yield f\"clean: {name}\", schema\n        for name, row in SINGLE_DEFECT_SCHEMAS.items():\n            yield f\"single: {name}\", row[0]\n        for name, (schema, _) in MULTI_DEFECT_SCHEMAS.items():\n            yield f\"multi: {name}\", schema\n        for name, schema in {\n            \"a bare true\": True,\n            \"a bare false\": False,\n            \"a string in schema position\": \"not a schema\",\n            \"None in schema position\": None,\n        }.items():\n            yield f\"malformed: {name}\", schema\n\n    def test_the_two_agree_in_both_directions_for_every_schema(self):\n        for name, schema in self._all_schemas():\n            for instance in PROBE_INSTANCES:\n                with self.subTest(schema=name, instance=instance):\n                    empty = _audit(schema) == []\n                    raised = _validate_raises(instance, schema)\n                    self.assertEqual(\n                        empty, not raised,\n                        f\"{name}: audit_schema returned \"\n                        f\"{'no' if empty else 'some'} entries but validate \"\n                        f\"{'raised' if raised else 'did not raise'}; the two \"\n                        f\"must agree exactly\")\n\n    def test_an_empty_audit_means_validate_accepts_the_schema(self):\n        for name, schema in self._all_schemas():\n            if _audit(schema) != []:\n                continue\n            with self.subTest(schema=name):\n                for instance in PROBE_INSTANCES:\n                    self.assertFalse(\n                        _validate_raises(instance, schema),\n                        f\"{name}: the audit called it enforceable, so the \"\n                        f\"gate must not refuse it\")\n\n    def test_a_non_empty_audit_means_validate_refuses_the_schema(self):\n        for name, schema in self._all_schemas():\n            if _audit(schema) == []:\n                continue\n            with self.subTest(schema=name):\n                for instance in PROBE_INSTANCES:\n                    self.assertTrue(\n                        _validate_raises(instance, schema),\n                        f\"{name}: the audit found an unenforceable construct, \"\n                        f\"so the gate must refuse it\")\n\n\nclass AuditDiscriminationTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    \"\"\"The two wrong implementations this file exists to catch.\n\n    1. **The silent stub.** An ``audit_schema`` that returns ``[]`` for\n       everything. It is the current behaviour dressed up -- every schema\n       \"enforceable\", nothing located, nothing named -- and it is the most\n       likely wrong implementation, because it passes any test that only checks\n       that the clean cases are clean. Killed by asserting a non-empty audit\n       for every defective schema in the table, and by the iff above, which\n       such a stub breaks on every defective row.\n\n    2. **The first-defect-only stub.** An ``audit_schema`` that wraps the\n       existing eager walk in a ``try``/``except`` and returns one entry. It\n       looks right on every single-defect case and reproduces the exact failure\n       this row was raised to fix: three defective nodes, one named per run,\n       repeated raise-and-fix cycles. Killed by asserting the full count on the\n       multi-defect cases, the real-manifest case among them.\n\n    Both mistakes are asserted here directly rather than only as a consequence\n    of the tests above, so a future edit that softens those tests still has to\n    walk past this class.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_a_stub_that_always_returns_empty_is_refused(self):\n        defective = [row[0] for row in SINGLE_DEFECT_SCHEMAS.values()]\n        defective += [schema for schema, _ in MULTI_DEFECT_SCHEMAS.values()]\n        reported = 0\n        for schema in defective:\n            if _audit(schema):\n                reported += 1\n        self.assertEqual(\n            reported, len(defective),\n            \"every schema in the defect table carries at least one construct \"\n            \"the checker cannot enforce; an audit that returns [] for any of \"\n            \"them is the silence this module exists to prevent\")\n\n    def test_a_stub_that_reports_only_the_first_defect_is_refused(self):\n        for name, (schema, expected) in MULTI_DEFECT_SCHEMAS.items():\n            if len(expected) < 2:\n                continue\n            with self.subTest(schema=name):\n                entries = _audit(schema)\n                self.assertGreaterEqual(\n                    len(entries), len(expected),\n                    f\"{name} carries {len(expected)} unenforceable \"\n                    f\"constructs; That left it enforcing eleven keywords -- and left\n6: *both* real schema files in this repo raising, because both use constructs the\n7: checker does not implement. An honest checker that refuses everything you point\n8: it at is honest and useless.\n9: \n10: This row's purpose is to make both real schema files genuinely checkable:\n11: \n12: * ``$defs`` / ``$ref``, internal pointers only. An **external** ``$ref`` cannot\n13:   be fetched, so it must keep raising; a ``$ref`` that does not resolve must\n14:   raise too, because treating a broken pointer as \"no constraint\" is the exact\n15:   silence this module exists to end, wearing a different hat.\n16: * ``allOf`` -- every branch holds, and every branch's violations are reported.\n17: * ``if`` / ``then`` -- ``then`` applies only when ``if`` is satisfied. A failing\n18:   ``if`` is a *condition*, not a violation. ``else`` is out of scope: it is\n19:   unused in this repo and must keep raising.\n20: * ``oneOf`` -- exactly one branch. Zero is a violation and so is two or more,\n21:   and the two messages must be distinguishable.\n22: * ``not`` -- the subschema must not hold.\n23: * ``propertyNames`` -- every key of an object satisfies a subschema.\n24: * ``minItems``, ``minProperties``, ``maximum`` -- the obvious bounds, with\n25:   ``maximum`` mirroring ``minimum`` including that a boolean is not a number.\n26: * ``additionalProperties`` in its **subschema** form -- every property not named\n27:   in ``properties`` must satisfy that subschema. Now that composition is implemented, an unsupported\n681:     keyword or an unenforceable form can hide inside an ``allOf`` branch, a\n682:     ``oneOf`` branch, a ``not``, an ``if``, a ``then``, a ``propertyNames``, an\n683:     ``additionalProperties`` subschema, or a ``$defs`` definition. An\n684:     implementation whose audit stops at the composition boundary reports a clean\n685:     bill of health for a subschema it never looked at -- the same silence, one\n686:     level further in.\n687:     \"\"\"\n688: \n689:     def test_an_unsupported_keyword_hiding_in_composition_raises(self):\n690:         for site, schema in _sites(NAME_DEFECT).items():\n691:             with self.subTest(site=site):\n692:                 self.assert_raises_naming(\"totallyBogusKeyword\", {}, schema)\n693: \n694:     def test_an_unenforceable_form_hiding_in_composition_raises(self):\n695:         for keyword, defect in SHAPE_DEFECTS.items():\n696:             for site, schema in _sites(defect).items():\n697:                 with self.subTest(keyword=keyword, site=site):\n698:                     self.assert_raises_naming(keyword, {}, schema)\n699: \n700:     def test_a_boolean_subschema_hiding_in_composition_raises(self):\n701:         for site, schema in _sites(True).items():\n702:             if site.startswith(\"additionalProperties\"):\n703:                 # That left it enforcing eleven keywords -- and left\n*both* real schema files in this repo raising, because both use constructs the\nchecker does not implement. An honest checker that refuses everything you point\nit at is honest and useless.\n\nThis row's purpose is to make both real schema files genuinely checkable:\n\n* ``$defs`` / ``$ref``, internal pointers only. An **external** ``$ref`` cannot\n  be fetched, so it must keep raising; a ``$ref`` that does not resolve must\n  raise too, because treating a broken pointer as \"no constraint\" is the exact\n  silence this module exists to end, wearing a different hat.\n* ``allOf`` -- every branch holds, and every branch's violations are reported.\n* ``if`` / ``then`` -- ``then`` applies only when ``if`` is satisfied. A failing\n  ``if`` is a *condition*, not a violation. ``else`` is out of scope: it is\n  unused in this repo and must keep raising.\n* ``oneOf`` -- exactly one branch. Zero is a violation and so is two or more,\n  and the two messages must be distinguishable.\n* ``not`` -- the subschema must not hold.\n* ``propertyNames`` -- every key of an object satisfies a subschema.\n* ``minItems``, ``minProperties``, ``maximum`` -- the obvious bounds, with\n  ``maximum`` mirroring ``minimum`` including that a boolean is not a number.\n* ``additionalProperties`` in its **subschema** form -- every property not named\n  in ``properties`` must satisfy that subschema. The other three forms are\n  unchanged: ``False`` forbids extras and names each one, ``True`` and absent\n  constrain nothing.\n\nBecause the subschema form is now enforced, ``additionalProperties`` drops out\nof the *shape* audit entirely -- all four of its forms are handled. The other\nthree shape rules are untouched and still raise: ``items`` as a positional\nlist, ``type`` as a union list, and a bare boolean in schema position.\n\nThree things this file exists to hold, beyond the keyword list:\n\n1. **The audit follows composition.** The eager, total audit is the module's\n   core guarantee: it walks the whole schema, including branches no instance\n   reaches, because the question is whether the schema is honestly enforced.\n   Once these keywords are handled the audit must descend into all of them --\n   ``allOf`` branches, ``oneOf`` branches, ``not``, ``if``, ``then``,\n   ``propertyNames``, an ``additionalProperties`` subschema, and every ``$defs``\n   definition, referenced or not. An implementation that handles composition at\n   validation time but not at audit time reintroduces the silence one level in.\n\n2. **The payoff is real, and it is both files.** ``task-packet.schema.json``\n   must return a verdict and every committed packet must validate against it\n   with zero violations. ``manifest.schema.json`` must return a verdict too --\n   its three ``additionalProperties`` subschema nodes become enforced\n   constraints, and one of them\n   (``instruction_set.properties.skill_lock.oneOf[0]``) is reachable *only*\n   through ``oneOf``. So both the audit and the checker must follow ``oneOf``,\n   and ``RealManifestSchemaTests`` pins that with an instance that can only be\n   rejected if the constraint behind ``oneOf`` is actually applied.\n\n3. **Recognising a keyword is not enforcing it.** See\n   ``CompositionDiscriminationTests``.\n\nConventions follow ``test_schema_check.py``: the subject is loaded with\nimportlib, nothing is asserted about message *wording* (only breadcrumbs and\noffending names, via ``assertIn``), and every \"this must raise\" claim about a\nkeyword outside this row's scope is conditioned on ``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS``\nmembership so a later row can implement it without editing this file.\n\nKNOWN CONFLICT, deliberately not papered over: seven tests in the previous\nrow's oracle (``test_schema_check.py``) assert that the *subschema* form of\n``additionalProperties`` raises, three of them unconditionally, and that\n``manifest.schema.json`` is refused. This row makes both false on purpose. That\nfile needs a coordinator hand-pass; this oracle does not restate its claims and\ncannot satisfy them.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport copy\nimport importlib.util\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport unittest\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nsys.dont_write_bytecode = True\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).parents[3]\nSCRIPTS = ROOT / \"templates/dispatch/scripts\"\nTASK_PACKET_SCHEMA_PATH = ROOT / \"templates/dispatch/hybrid/task-packet.schema.json\"\nMANIFEST_SCHEMA_PATH = ROOT / \"templates/dispatch/manifest.schema.json\"\nDISPATCH_MANIFEST_PATH = ROOT / \"agentops.dispatch.json\"\nPACKET_DIR = ROOT / \"docs/evidence/packets\"\n\n\ndef _load(name: str, path: Path):\n    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(name, path)\n    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\n    assert spec.loader is not None\n    spec.loader.exec_module(module)\n    return module\n\n\nschema_check = _load(\"schema_check_composition_subject\", SCRIPTS / \"schema_check.py\")\n\nvalidate = schema_check.validate\nUnsupportedKeyword = schema_check.UnsupportedKeyword\n\n#: The keywords this row adds. Now that composition is implemented, an unsupported\n    keyword or an unenforceable form can hide inside an ``allOf`` branch, a\n    ``oneOf`` branch, a ``not``, an ``if``, a ``then``, a ``propertyNames``, an\n    ``additionalProperties`` subschema, or a ``$defs`` definition. An\n    implementation whose audit stops at the composition boundary reports a clean\n    bill of health for a subschema it never looked at -- the same silence, one\n    level further in.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_an_unsupported_keyword_hiding_in_composition_raises(self):\n        for site, schema in _sites(NAME_DEFECT).items():\n            with self.subTest(site=site):\n                self.assert_raises_naming(\"totallyBogusKeyword\", {}, schema)\n\n    def test_an_unenforceable_form_hiding_in_composition_raises(self):\n        for keyword, defect in SHAPE_DEFECTS.items():\n            for site, schema in _sites(defect).items():\n                with self.subTest(keyword=keyword, site=site):\n                    self.assert_raises_naming(keyword, {}, schema)\n\n    def test_a_boolean_subschema_hiding_in_composition_raises(self):\n        for site, schema in _sites(True).items():\n            if site.startswith(\"additionalProperties\"):\n                # `additionalProperties: true` is a legal, meaningful form\n                # (\"no constraint\"), not a boolean standing in for a schema.\n                continue\n            with self.subTest(site=site):\n                with self.assertRaises(UnsupportedKeyword):\n                    validate({}, schema)\n\n    def test_an_out_of_scope_keyword_hiding_in_composition_raises(self):\n        for keyword in STILL_UNIMPLEMENTED:\n            if keyword in schema_check.SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS:\n                continue\n            defect = {\"type\": \"object\", keyword: {}}\n            for site, schema in _sites(defect).items():\n                with self.subTest(keyword=keyword, site=site):\n                    self.assert_raises_naming(keyword, {}, schema)\n\n    def test_the_audit_runs_even_when_the_instance_fails_early(self):\n        # A type violation at the root stops validation descending. It must not\n        # stop the audit: the schema is what is being audited.\n        schema = {\"type\": \"object\",\n                  \"properties\": {\"a\": {\"allOf\": [NAME_DEFECT]}}}\n        self.assert_raises_naming(\"totallyBogusKeyword\", \"not an object\", schema)\n\n    def test_a_clean_composition_schema_does_not_raise(self):\n        # The counterweight: the audit must not have become a blanket refusal.\n        schema = {\n            \"type\": \"object\",\n            \"properties\": {\"a\": {\"$ref\": \"#/$defs/word\"}},\n            \"$defs\": {\"word\": {\"type\": \"string\", \"minLength\": 1}},\n            \"additionalProperties\": {\"type\": \"integer\"},\n            \"allOf\": [{\"required\": [\"a\"]}],\n            \"oneOf\": [{\"required\": [\"a\"]}, {\"required\": [\"zzz\"]}],\n            \"not\": {\"required\": [\"forbidden\"]},\n            \"if\": {\"required\": [\"a\"]},\n            \"then\": {\"minProperties\": 1},\n            \"propertyNames\": {\"pattern\": \"^[a-z]+$\"},\n        }\n        self.assertEqual(validate({\"a\": \"x\"}, schema), [])\n\n\nclass RealTaskPacketSchemaTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    \"\"\"Payoff, part one: the packet schema becomes enforceable, end to end.\n\n    ``task-packet.schema.json`` uses ``$defs``, ``$ref``, ``allOf``,\n    ``if``/``then``, ``minItems`` and ``maximum``. Before this row the checker\n    refused it outright, so nothing in this repo could actually be validated\n    against the schema it declares. After this row it must return a verdict --\n    and the verdict on every committed packet must be clean.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @classmethod\n    def setUpClass(cls):\n        assert TASK_PACKET_SCHEMA_PATH.is_file(), TASK_PACKET_SCHEMA_PATH\n        cls.schema = json.loads(\n            TASK_PACKET_SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        cls.packet_paths = sorted(PACKET_DIR.glob(\"*.json\"))\n\n    def test_there_are_packets_to_check(self):\n        # Guards the whole class against passing vacuously if the directory\n        # moves or the glob stops matching.\n        self.assertTrue(\n            self.packet_paths,\n            f\"no packets found under {PACKET_DIR}; the end-to-end proof below \"\n            f\"would pass by matching nothing\")\n\n    def test_the_schema_still_uses_the_composition_keywords(self):\n        # The anchor for this class. If the schema is ever rewritten without\n        # composition, the tests below stop proving anything -- and this one\n        # says so out loud instead of passing quietly.\n        self.assertIn(\"$defs\", self.schema)\n        self.assertIsInstance(self.schema[\"allOf\"], list)\n        self.assertTrue(self.schema[\"allOf\"])\n        first = self.schema[\"allOf\"][0]\n        self.assertIn(\"if\", first)\n        self.assertIn(\"then\", first)\n        self.assertIn(\n            \"$ref\", json.dumps(first[\"then\"]),\n            \"the conditional branch is expected to reach a $defs definition\")\n\n    def test_the_schema_returns_a_verdict_instead_of_raising(self):\n        result = validate({}, self.schema)\n        self.assertIsInstance(\n            result, list,\n            \"the packet schema must be checkable after this row; refusing it \"\n            \"is what made the checker useless\")\n\n    def test_every_committed_packet_validates_with_zero_violations(self):\n        for packet_path in self.packet_paths:\n            with self.subTest(packet=packet_path.name):\n                packet = json.loads(packet_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n                self.assertEqual(\n                    validate(packet, self.schema), [],\n                    f\"{packet_path.name} does not satisfy \"\n                    f\"task-packet.schema.json\")\n\n    def _a_real_packet(self):\n        return copy.deepcopy(\n            json.loads(self.packet_paths[0].read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")))\n\n    def test_the_conditional_defs_branch_actually_bites(self):\n        # allOf -> if -> then -> $ref, exercised against the real file. Every\n        # committed packet is schema_version v2, whose acceptance properties\n        # must carry a stable `id`. Remove it and the v2 branch must object.\n        packet = self._a_real_packet()\n        self.assertEqual(packet[\"schema_version\"], \"agentops-task/v2\")\n        self.assertTrue(packet[\"acceptance_properties\"])\n        removed = packet[\"acceptance_properties\"][0].pop(\"id\", None)\n        self.assertIsNotNone(\n            removed, \"the anchor: a v2 acceptance property carries an id\")\n        self.assertTrue(\n            validate(packet, self.schema),\n            \"the v2 branch of the schema's allOf/if/then/$ref requires an id \"\n            \"on every acceptance property; a checker that reports [] here has \"\n            \"walked past the whole composition\")\n\n    def test_the_other_conditional_branch_selects_differently(self):\n        # Same packet, relabelled v1. The v1 definition is closed and has no\n        # `id`, so the *other* branch must now object -- which is only possible\n        # if `if` really is discriminating on schema_version.\n        packet = self._a_real_packet()\n        packet[\"schema_version\"] = \"agentops-task/v1\"\n        self.assertTrue(\n            validate(packet, self.schema),\n            \"a v2-shaped acceptance property under the v1 label must be \"\n            \"rejected by the v1 definition\")\n\n    def test_a_real_maximum_in_the_schema_bites(self):\n        packet = self._a_real_packet()\n        ceiling = self.schema[\"properties\"][\"limits\"][\"properties\"][\n            \"timeout_seconds\"][\"maximum\"]\n        packet[\"limits\"][\"timeout_seconds\"] = ceiling + 1\n        self.assertTrue(\n            validate(packet, self.schema),\n            \"timeout_seconds carries a maximum in the real schema\")\n\n    def test_a_real_min_items_in_the_schema_bites(self):\n        packet = self._a_real_packet()\n        self.assertEqual(\n            self.schema[\"properties\"][\"readable_context_paths\"][\"minItems\"], 1)\n        packet[\"readable_context_paths\"] = []\n        self.assertTrue(\n            validate(packet, self.schema),\n            \"readable_context_paths carries minItems 1 in the real schema\")\n\n\nclass RealManifestSchemaTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    \"\"\"Payoff, part two: ``manifest.schema.json`` becomes checkable too.\n\n    It carries the subschema form of ``additionalProperties`` in three nodes.\n    Two are plain ``properties`` descents; This\n    class was written while ``agentops.dispatch.json`` was NOT clean --\n    ``skills.selected`` carried ``dispatch-wave`` and ``session-handover``,\n    which the schema's enum did not admit. That drift is what this whole tract\n    set out to find, and pinning it was right while it stood. It works, it caught real drift in the packet\nschema, and it cannot be imported by any script -- so the second consumer that\nnow needs it would have to copy it, which is how this repo ended up with\n``_path_allowed`` and ``_matches_any`` answering one question twice.\n\nThis row extracts that checker into a module and fixes the lie in its\ndocstring. The docstring claimed unimplemented keywords were \"ignored rather\nthan silently passed off as checked\"; in fact they were ignored *silently*, and\nthe two schemas it is pointed at already use keywords it does not implement. A\nconstraint the checker skips is a constraint that does not exist. So the module\nmust refuse: a schema keyword that is neither enforced (``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS``)\nnor knowingly constraint-free (``ANNOTATION_KEYWORDS``) raises\n``UnsupportedKeyword``, naming the keyword.\n\nScope note for whoever reads this next: this row does NOT implement the\ncomposition keywords (``allOf``, ``$ref``, ``if``/``then``, ``oneOf``, ``not``,\n``propertyNames``, ``minItems``, ``minProperties``, ``maximum``). Today they\nmust raise. A later row implements them. This file is therefore written to pin\n*behaviour conditioned on ``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS`` membership*, never on a frozen\nliteral keyword list: an oracle that made the next row impossible to land would\nbe a badly designed oracle, but an oracle that let an implementation quietly\ndrop a keyword would be worse. The bargain is:\n\n* a keyword the module claims to support must actually be enforced (the\n  eleven-keyword floor below, plus a satisfy/violate pair for each);\n* a keyword the module does not claim must raise;\n* a constraint-bearing keyword must never be parked in ANNOTATION_KEYWORDS.\n\nGrowing ``SUPPORTED_KEYWORDS`` is legal and this file follows it. Emptying the\nenforcement out from under a supported keyword is not, and this file fails.\n\nThe audit is on shapes as well as names. A keyword the checker implements can\nstill appear in a *form* it does not enforce -- ``additionalProperties`` as a\nsubschema, ``items`` as a positional list, ``type`` as a union list, a boolean\nused where a schema object belongs. A name-level audit waves all four through\nand the skipped constraint is silent again, one level further down, which is\nthe same defect wearing a different hat. So those raise too, with the same\nexception, eagerly, at any depth.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport importlib.util\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport unittest\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nsys.dont_write_bytecode = True\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).parents[3]\nSCRIPTS = ROOT / \"templates/dispatch/scripts\"\nTASK_PACKET_SCHEMA_PATH = ROOT / \"templates/dispatch/hybrid/task-packet.schema.json\"\nMANIFEST_SCHEMA_PATH = ROOT / \"templates/dispatch/manifest.schema.json\"\n\n\ndef _load(name: str, path: Path):\n    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(name, path)\n    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\n    assert spec.loader is not None\n    spec.loader.exec_module(module)\n    return module\n\n\nschema_check = _load(\"schema_check_subject\", SCRIPTS / \"schema_check.py\")\n\nvalidate = schema_check.validate\nUnsupportedKeyword = schema_check.UnsupportedKeyword\n\n#: The keywords this row must enforce. A checker that\n        # only notices keywords it happens to walk past would still be lying\n        # about the optional branch it never visited.\n        if _handled(\"minItems\"):\n            self.skipTest(\"minItems is now handled\")\n        schema = {\n            \"type\": \"object\",\n            \"properties\": {\n                \"present\": {\"type\": \"string\"},\n                \"absent\": {\"type\": \"array\", \"minItems\": 1},\n            },\n        }\n        self._assert_raises_naming(\"minItems\", {\"present\": \"x\"}, schema)\n\n    def test_a_defs_style_container_is_not_a_hiding_place(self):\n        if _handled(\"$defs\"):\n            self.skipTest(\"$defs is now handled\")\n        self._assert_raises_naming(\n            \"$defs\", \"x\",\n            {\"type\": \"string\", \"$defs\": {\"thing\": {\"type\": \"string\"}}})\n\n    def test_annotation_keywords_do_not_raise_and_add_no_constraint(self):\n        schema = {\n            \"$schema\": \"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema\",\n            \"$id\": \"https://agentops.local/schemas/example.schema.json\",\n            \"$comment\": \"not a constraint\",\n            \"title\": \"Example\",\n            \"description\": \"Annotations carry no constraint.\",\n            \"default\": \"fallback\",\n            \"examples\": [\"one\", \"two\"],\n            \"deprecated\": False,\n            \"format\": \"uuid\",\n            \"type\": \"string\",\n        }\n        # This checker\n        # does not implement either, so it must not pretend to.\n        for boolean_schema in (True, False):\n            with self.subTest(schema=boolean_schema):\n                with self.assertRaises(UnsupportedKeyword):\n                    validate(\"x\", boolean_schema)\n\n    def test_a_boolean_subschema_raises(self):\n        for schema in ({\"type\": \"object\", \"properties\": {\"a\": True}},\n                       {\"type\": \"array\", \"items\": False}):\n            with self.subTest(schema=schema):\n                with self.assertRaises(UnsupportedKeyword):\n                    validate({} if schema[\"type\"] == \"object\" else [], schema)\n\n    def test_an_unenforceable_form_raises_where_the_instance_never_reaches_it(self):\n        # Same eager rule as the name audit: the schema is what is audited.\n        for keyword, (bad, _instance) in SHAPE_CASES.items():\n            with self.subTest(keyword=keyword):\n                schema = {\n                    \"type\": \"object\",\n                    \"properties\": {\"present\": {\"type\": \"string\"}, \"absent\": bad},\n                }\n                self._assert_raises_naming(keyword, {\"present\": \"x\"}, schema)\n\n    def test_an_unenforceable_form_raises_inside_items(self):\n        # Exemplified with the union form of `type`, which stays unenforceable.\n        # It was `additionalProperties` as a subschema until V5.9-2 implemented\n        # that form; the guarantee being pinned -- a bad form nested inside a\n        # container still raises -- is unchanged, and is now pinned by a case\n        # that no planned row will implement away.\n        self._assert_raises_naming(\n            \"type\", [],\n            {\"type\": \"array\",\n             \"items\": {\"type\": \"object\",\n                       \"properties\": {\"a\": {\"type\": [\"string\", \"null\"]}}}})\n\n    def test_the_enforced_forms_still_do_not_raise(self):\n        for schema, instance in (\n                ({\"type\": \"object\", \"additionalProperties\": False,\n                  \"properties\": {\"a\": {\"type\": \"string\"}}}, {\"a\": \"x\"}),\n                ({\"type\": \"array\", \"items\": {\"type\": \"string\"}}, [\"x\"]),\n                ({\"type\": \"string\"}, \"x\")):\n            with self.subTest(schema=schema):\n                self.assertEqual(validate(instance, schema), [])\n\n    def test_additional_properties_true_imposes_nothing_and_does_not_raise(self):\n        # `true` and absent both mean \"no constraint\", so ignoring them is\n        # honest -- there is nothing being skipped.\n        schema = {\"type\": \"object\", \"additionalProperties\": True,\n                  \"properties\": {\"a\": {\"type\": \"string\"}}}\n        self.assertEqual(validate({\"a\": \"x\", \"extra\": 1}, schema), [])\n\n    def test_a_shape_violation_uses_the_same_exception(self):\n        # Callers catch one thing, whether the schema named a keyword the\n        # checker lacks or used one in a form it cannot enforce.\n        with self.assertRaises(UnsupportedKeyword):\n            validate([], {\"type\": \"array\", \"items\": [{\"type\": \"string\"}]})\n\n    def test_the_subschema_form_is_enforced_or_refused_but_never_ignored(self):\n        # The two acceptable answers for additionalProperties as a subschema,\n        # and the one that is never acceptable. That is the point:\n    the checker must say so out loud rather than validate them and report an\n    empty list it has no right to. When a later row implements those keywords,\n    these tests follow it into the enforced branch.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def _schema(self, path):\n        self.assertTrue(path.is_file(), f\"missing schema: {path}\")\n        return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n    #: Keywords both real schemas use that this row does not implement.
